```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_5/projects/structfuse-context/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — StructFuse\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main\\n- Deadline: ARR May 25, 2026\\n\\n## Core Claims (3)\\n\\n1. **True embedding-distance hard negative mining consistently improves contrastive code retrieval over random baselines, correcting the prior misconception that embedding negatives harm performance.** The reported \\\"harm\\\" (CoCoHaNeRe-derived, -27pp R@1) was an artifact of BM25-proxy negatives mislabeled as embedding-distance — genuine embedding mining yields +1.6–2.0pp R@1 across two architecturally distinct encoders (Qwen2.5-Coder-3B, StarCoder2-3B).\\n\\n2. **Structure-grounded (dataflow) hard negatives provide conditional additional benefit beyond embedding mining, mediated by encoder representation saturation.** When pretrained encoder representations are compressed (pos_sim > 0.98, as in Qwen), random and embedding negatives become too easy; dataflow-disconnected negatives force discrimination along a structural dimension the encoder cannot separate via text alone. When representation space is healthy (pos_sim ~ 0.80, as in StarCoder), embedding mining alone suffices. Mechanism: neg_difficulty margin analysis (+17% Qwen, +28% StarCoder) provides quantitative evidence for this interaction.\\n\\n3. **Scope-bounded inter-procedural data-flow analysis via CodeQL is a scalable, model-independent method for computing structural connectivity.** 0% timeout, 78% connected-not-gold rate (Z%), 123/131 repos with output at full scale. Method is a general-purpose structural negative generator applicable beyond code (molecular networks, circuit topology, KG retrieval).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Repo-level code repair requires selecting relevant files from large codebases. Current retrievers rely on text similarity alone, but lexical similarity ≠ structural relevance. Hard negative mining is the standard approach to improve contrastive retrievers, yet the field lacks systematic understanding of what negative selection strategies work for code and why.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**:\\n  - Text-based retrieval (SweRank, SWE-Fixer): blind to structural dependencies\\n  - Graph-guided at inference (LocAgent, RANGER, CodexGraph): structural graphs at inference only, no training signal\\n  - ARISE: data-flow but intra-procedural only (no call boundary crossing), runtime tool calls only\\n  - CoCoHaNeRe: reports embedding negatives harm code search — but uses BM25 proxy, not true embedding distance\\n  - Gap: no systematic study comparing negative mining strategies with proper controls, and no mechanistic explanation for when/why each strategy works\\n\\n- **Our contribution**: A systematic analysis of hard negative mining strategies for contrastive code retrieval. We (1) correct the misconception that embedding negatives harm performance (BM25 proxy ≠ embedding distance), (2) introduce structure-grounded negatives via inter-procedural dataflow and characterize when they provide additional benefit, and (3) provide mechanistic explanation via representation space saturation analysis.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"True embedding-distance mining consistently improves code retrieval (+1.6–2.0pp R@1, 2 encoders), correcting prior reports of harm. Structure-grounded (dataflow) negatives provide further conditional benefit (+0.8–2.3pp) when encoder representations are saturated, with the interaction mechanism quantified via negative difficulty analysis.\\\"\\n\\n## Core Insight\\n\\nThe effectiveness of hard negative mining strategies depends on the encoder's representation geometry. When the pretrained space is saturated (high pos_sim → low margin between positive and negative), the encoder needs harder negatives from an orthogonal signal dimension (structure). When the space has healthy separation, embedding-distance mining alone captures sufficient difficulty gradient. This encoder-strategy interaction explains contradictory findings in prior work.\\n\\n## Dual-Pillar Motivation\\n\\n1. **Primary — Systematic analysis correcting misconceptions**: Prior work (CoCoHaNeRe) conflated BM25-proxy negatives with true embedding-distance negatives, leading to incorrect conclusions about hard negative mining for code. Our controlled comparison isolates the effect of each strategy.\\n2. **Secondary — Mechanistic explanation via representation geometry**: neg_difficulty analysis (margin distribution × strategy × encoder) provides explanatory depth beyond empirical comparison, enabling practitioners to predict which strategy suits their encoder without exhaustive experiments.\\n\\n## Formal Definition\\n\\nGiven retrieval domain D, structural graph G, similarity function s, query q:\\n**structure-grounded hard negative** = argmax_{x ∈ D : s(x,q) > τ ∧ ¬reachable_G(x,q)} s(x,q)\\n\\nThe structural graph is externally fixed and does not update with the encoder (drift-immune).\\n\\n## Title Options\\n\\n- Primary: \\\"What Makes Hard Negatives Work for Code Retrieval? A Systematic Study of Mining Strategies and Representation Geometry\\\"\\n- Alternative: \\\"StructFuse: When and Why Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives Improve Contrastive Code Retrieval\\\"\\n- Backup: \\\"Correcting Misconceptions in Hard Negative Mining for Code: Embedding Distance Helps, Structure Helps Conditionally\\\"\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n1. **EMNLP Main fit**: Empirical analysis + mechanistic insight papers are a strong EMNLP archetype (cf. \\\"Do Language Models...\\\" papers). Contribution is understanding, not just a new method.\\n2. **NLP audience**: Frame as contrastive learning methodology with code as instantiation. Representation geometry analysis generalizes beyond code.\\n3. **Against \\\"incremental\\\" criticism**: Paper corrects a misconception + provides mechanistic explanation + introduces new negative mining paradigm. Three contributions, not one marginal improvement.\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n| Concern | Response | Evidence |\\n|---------|----------|----------|\\n| Only 2 encoders — can you generalize? | n=2 is minimum for interaction claim; we frame as observation with mechanism, not universal law; cross-architecture (decoder vs encoder-decoder) strengthens it | plan_013 (done), m2_full runs |\\n| Temperature regime specificity | Ablate τ ∈ {0.05, 0.1, 0.2} | plan_014 |\\n| No downstream task evaluation | SWE-bench Lite end-to-end with LLM agent | plan_003 |\\n| Static vs dynamic mining confound | Clarify mining is static; add dynamic baseline | plan_015 |\\n| CodeQL scalability at larger scale | 0% timeout, 78% Z%, 123 repos; m1_reextract_v2 adds more | plan_006 (done) |\\n| Why not compare inference-time graph methods? | Complementary: they navigate at inference, we mine at training. Discuss relationship. | plan_017 |\\n| Single benchmark | Add RepoBench-R cross-file retrieval | plan_004 |\\n| Statistical significance | Bootstrap 95% CI for all comparisons; full M2 = 9 seeds × 3 strategies × 2 encoders | plan_016, m2_full |\\n| \\\"Complementary signal\\\" overstatement | Temper: \\\"conditionally beneficial\\\" not \\\"always complementary\\\" | Writing discipline |\\n| Missing related work | RepoFusion, GRACE, GraphCodeBERT, ANCE, curriculum HN mining | Writing task |\\n\\n## Evidence Status\\n\\n### Confirmed findings (basis for claims):\\n- D017: True embedding mining helps (+2.0pp Qwen, +1.6pp StarCoder) — corrects MVP BM25-proxy artifact\\n- D021/neg_difficulty: Qwen pos_sim=0.9855 (saturated), StarCoder pos_sim=0.7985 (healthy); margin differential explains strategy interaction\\n- m2_embed_verify (pilot): 3-strategy comparison confirms ranking (embedding > random, dataflow conditional)\\n- plan_013 (StarCoder pilot): Confirms cross-encoder pattern — embedding helps both, dataflow helps Qwen only\\n- plan_006: CodeQL scalability confirmed at 123-repo scale\\n\\n### In progress:\\n- m2_full_qwen: 9 seeds × 3 strategies (seed 1 running, best R@1=55.3% at step 1000)\\n- m2_full_starcoder: 9 seeds × 3 strategies (seed 1 running, R@1=45.4% at step 1000, still improving)\\n- m1_reextract_v2: expanded extraction for higher coverage\\n\\n### Planned (priority order):\\n- plan_003: SWE-bench Lite eval (biggest lever for paper strength)\\n- plan_014: Temperature ablation (Dream reviewer concern)\\n- plan_004: RepoBench-R second benchmark\\n- plan_015: Dynamic mining baseline\\n- plan_007/008/009: Additional ablations\\n\\n## Contingency (if full M2 results weaken claims)\\n\\n### All 18 runs confirm pilot pattern: Best case\\n- Full statistical power, paper as framed above\\n- Target: EMNLP Main\\n\\n### Dataflow benefit disappears even for Qwen at full scale: Pivot to 2-claim paper\\n- Drop Claim 2 to analysis observation\\n- Paper becomes: \\\"True embedding mining helps (correcting misconception) + CodeQL extraction is practical + representation geometry analysis explains prior contradictions\\\"\\n- Still publishable: correcting misconception + mechanistic analysis is sufficient for EMNLP Main\\n\\n### Embedding mining also loses advantage at full scale: Major reassessment needed\\n- Recheck data pipeline for bugs (full-scale data may have distribution differences)\\n- If confirmed: negative result paper — \\\"Why Hard Negative Mining Doesn't Scale for Code Retrieval\\\"\\n- Discuss with user before committing direction\\n\\n## Deprecated Claims (with reason)\\n\\n- ~~\\\"Embedding-distance hard negatives actively harm code retrieval\\\"~~ — FALSIFIED by D017. Original evidence (-27pp R@1) was from BM25-proxy negatives incorrectly labeled as embedding_distance in MVP. True embedding mining shows consistent improvement. Preserved here to prevent regression.\\n\", \"total_chars\": 9391, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

同方向同显著 → 强泛化 claim\\\\n\\\\n    - 同方向不同显著 → 条件 claim + architecture discussion\\\\n\\\\n    - 反方向 → Claim 1 原形式被拒，需 conditional reframe\\\\n\\\\n\\\\n    阈值在 full M2 结果出来前锁定，不允许事后修改。'\\\\n  related_experiments:\\\\n  - 同方向同显著 → 强泛化 claim\\n\\n    - 同方向不同显著 → 条件 claim + architecture discussion\\n\\n    - 反方向 → Claim 1 原形式被拒，需 conditional reframe\\n\\n\\n    阈值在 full M2 结果出来前锁定，不允许事后修改。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - D018\\n- id: D020\\n  date: 2026-05-13 18:10 UTC\\n  summary: D019 补充：Claim 2 完整判定矩阵 + n=3 功效限制声明\\n  rationale: 'Reviewer 指出 D019 的 Claim 2 阈值不完整 + n=3 统计功效天然受限。补充如下：\\n\\n\\n    **Claim 2 完整判定矩阵**（3 seeds, paired bootstrap 95% CI）：\\n\\n    (i) random > embedding CI>0 → Claim 2 confirmed（embedding harms，与 MVP 一致）\\n\\n    (ii) embedding > random CI>0 → Claim 2 falsified（true embedding mining helps，需\\n    reframe）\\n\\n    (iii) CI 含 0 → inconclusive（效应存在但检测不到或确实无差异，论文报告为 \\\"no significant difference\\\"\\n    + 报告 effect size）\\n\\n\\n    **n=3 功效限制**（显式承认，不修改阈值）：\\n\\n    - df=2 时 t 临界值 4.303，pilot 级别差异（<2pp）极难达到 p<0.05\\n\\n    - Bootstrap n=3 仅 27 种重采样，CI 估计粗糙\\n\\n    - 阈值框架偏向 No-Go（Type II error 高）\\n\\n    - 若结果落入 (C) No-Go，论文 Discussion 必须讨论功效限制，不能简单声明 \\\"dataflow 无效\\\"\\n\\n    - 这是算力约束下的合理选择，不改阈值，但明确风险\\n\\n\\n    **plan_014 排期**：Full M2 完成后优先启动 plan_014（temperature ablation, ~10h）——无论 Claim\\n    2 reframe 成哪个方向，temperature regime-specificity 都需要回答。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m2_full_qwen\\n    role: primary\\n    result: RUNNING\\n  - exp_id: m2_full_starcoder\\n    role: primary\\n    result: RUNNING\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D019\\n- id: D021\\n  date: 2026-05-13 19:10 UTC\\n  summary: Negative difficulty 分析揭示 encoder capacity 差异是 dataflow 效果不一致的根因\\n  rationale: '预训练模型 cosine similarity 分析显示：Qwen pretrained embeddings 分辨力弱（random\\n    cosine sim 0.899），StarCoder 分辨力强（random cosine sim 0.553）。Dataflow negatives 在两个模型上都比\\n    random 更难（higher sim），说明方法设计正确。但 StarCoder 已经\\\"懂\\\"结构差异（所有策略 sim 都低），额外训练信号边际收益小。这解释了\\n    pilot 中 StarCoder dataflow < random 的现象：不是方法问题，是 encoder 已有足够 capacity。\\n\\n\\n    论文价值：(1) 从 negative result 中提取 mechanistic explanation；(2) 定义 method applicability\\n    condition：\\\"structure-grounded negatives benefit most when pretrained encoder has\\n    limited structural separability\\\"；(3) 产出论文级图表（Figure: Negative Difficulty Distributions）；(4)\\n    无论 full M2 落入 A/B/C，这个分析都增加论文深度。不修改 go/no-go 阈值，但丰富解读框架。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m2_embed_verify\\n    role: context\\n    result: 'Qwen: dataflow > random (+0.8~2.3pp)'\\n  - exp_id: plan_013_starcoder\\n    role: context\\n    result: 'StarCoder: dataflow < random (-0.5pp), explained by high pretrained separability'\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D018\\n  - D019\\n- id: D022\\n  date: 2026-05-13 19:25 UTC\\n  summary: M1 28K instances 可恢复（运行时故障非数据问题）；当前 M2 继续，并行后台重跑 M1 提取\\n  rationale: 'Fuzzy match 抽查 10/10 发现 buggy_file 全部存在——28,276 个 \\\"error\\\" 是 CodeQL 并行提取时的运行时故障（磁盘/存储瞬态），不是\\n    SWE-smith 数据质量问题。训练数据潜在从 15,897 → ~44,173（2.8x）。\\n\\n\\n    决策：不中断当前 M2 训练 + 并行后台重跑 M1 提取。理由：\\n\\n    (1) 当前 M2 已在双卡运行，中断重来延期 3-7 天超出 DDL\\n\\n    (2) M1 重提取是 CPU 工作，可在 bjb1-42106 后台运行不影响 GPU 训练\\n\\n    (3) 如果当前 M2 结果落入 (C) No-Go 或 effect size 太小，扩展数据集是 natural next step\\n\\n    (4) 2.8x 数据量可能显著缓解 dataflow 过拟合问题（D021 中的核心风险）\\n\\n\\n    Claim 3 叙事需修正：\\\"64% failure 是上游数据问题\\\" → \\\"64% 是运行时提取故障，CodeQL 本身在有效输入上 100% 成功，可通过重跑恢复\\\"。这是\\n    engineering limitation with clear fix，不是方法局限。'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m1_extraction\\n    role: primary\\n    result: 28,276 instances recoverable - runtime failure, not data quality\\n  - exp_id: plan_006_scalability\\n    role: affected\\n    result: 'error classification needs correction: buggy_file_not_found was misattributed'\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D018\\n  - D019\\n- id: D023\\n  date: 2026-05-13 19:43 UTC\\n  summary: Kill M1 重提取：bjb1 CodeQL 查询 100% 失败，D022 前提不成立需诊断后重新评估\\n  rationale: Reviewer CRITICAL 发现 bjb1-42106 上 CodeQL call_graph.ql 查询全部 exit status\\n    2，317/325 行失败，零有效结构数据产出。原 M1 在 cqa1-21977 成功，迁移后 CodeQL 版本/QL pack 可能不兼容。立即 kill\\n    进程释放 CPU/RAM，诊断 CodeQL 配置后决定修复或换服务器。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m1_reextract\\n    role: subject\\n    result: killed - CodeQL incompatible on bjb1\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D022\\n- id: D024\\n  date: 2026-05-14 00:49 UTC\\n  summary: 'Major paper framing rewrite: drop falsified \\\"embedding harms\\\" claim, pivot\\n    to \\\"systematic analysis + mechanistic insight\\\" narrative'\\n  rationale: 'Reviewer deep assessment confirmed Claim 2 (\\\"embedding actively harms\\\",\\n    -27pp) was built on BM25-proxy mislabeled as embedding-distance in MVP. D017 proved\\n    true embedding mining helps (+1.6-2.0pp). New framing: (1) embedding universally\\n    helps (corrects misconception), (2) dataflow conditionally helps (mediated by\\n    representation saturation), (3) CodeQL scalable. This is a stronger, more honest\\n    paper type for EMNLP — empirical analysis with mechanistic depth rather than false\\n    \\\"our method beats all\\\" claim.'\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m2_embed_verify\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: embedding > random confirmed for both encoders\\n  - exp_id: plan_013_starcoder\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: dataflow helps Qwen only, not StarCoder — conditional benefit\\n  - exp_id: neg_difficulty_analysis\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: pos_sim saturation explains strategy-encoder interaction\\n  depends_on: []\\n- id: D025\\n  date: 2026-05-14 01:01 UTC\\n  summary: Plan_014 temperature ablation 缩减为 2 strategies × 3τ × 2 epochs = 6 runs\\n    (~70h)，deadline 可达\\n  rationale: 原方案 9 runs × 3 epochs = 157h GPU，超出 May 25 ARR 截稿。缩减理由：(1) 回答 Dream reviewer\\n    的 regime-specificity concern 只需证明 embedding vs random 的相对优势跨 τ 一致，不需要 3 策略全跑；(2)\\n    M2 和 pilot 均显示 epoch 3 过拟合无收益，2 epochs 足够；(3) τ=0.07 已由 M2 覆盖，只需 {0.05, 0.1, 0.2}。6\\n    runs × ~11.7h = ~70h → 双卡并行 35h wall time，M2 完成后 ~1.5 天可跑完。Dataflow strategy 的\\n    τ ablation 降为 P2，时间充裕再补。\\n  related_experiments:\\n  - exp_id: m2_full_qwen\\n    role: evidence\\n    result: step 1000 best, epoch 3 overfitting → 2 epochs sufficient\\n  - exp_id: plan_014\\n    role: target\\n    result: budget revised from 10h to 70h, scope from 9 to 6 runs\\n  depends_on:\\n  - D024\\n\", \"total_chars\": 18667, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"

```text
Now I have a comprehensive view. Let me analyze the updated ROADMAP against the evidence.

**ROADMAP Review Points:**

### 1. Framing Direction (D024)
The ROADMAP says the new framing is: "Hard negative mining for code retrieval: what works, what doesn't, and why" — systematic analysis + mechanistic explanation. This is consistent with D024 and the updated paper_framing.md. The old falsified Claim 2 ("embedding harms") has been properly deprecated in paper_framing.md. ✅

### 2. Evidence Support for Claims
- **Claim 1 (embedding > random)**: Supported by pilot (D017, +1.6-2.0pp across both encoders). 
- **Claim 2 (dataflow conditional benefit)**: M2 full run is underway for statistical validation. Pilot shows Qwen positive, StarCoder neutral — still a conditional go. ✅ 
- **Claim 3 (CodeQL scalable)**: plan_006 completed with 0% timeout, 78% Z%, 123/131 repos. ✅

### 3. GPU Budget & Timeline
ROADMAP allocates cuda:0 to m2_full_qwen (seed 1/9, around step 1550/4242) and cuda:1 to m2_full_st, tracking progress against the planned schedule.

Looking at the remaining GPU hours needed for plan_003/014/004/015 — the bottleneck is really M2's completion time rather than total capacity. If the eval at step 2000 confirms we can reduce to 2 epochs, that saves roughly 96 hours total, pushing M2 completion to around May 18-19 instead of May 20-21. Based on the registry notes showing 11-14 hours per run, 9 sequential runs per encoder would take 99-126 hours normally, but cutting a third of the epochs brings that down to 66-84 hours per GPU.

Let me recalculate the actual timeline — seed 1 started May 13 at 17:39 UTC and is now around step 1850 out of 4242 total steps across 3 epochs, which is roughly 15.5 hours elapsed. Extrapolating that rate to the full 4242 steps gives about 35.5 hours per run, meaning 9 runs for Qwen alone would be around 320 hours — that's way longer than expected. I need to reconcile the step counts: the roadmap shows step 1550 at the last snapshot, but my latest log check shows 1850, so I'm working with slightly different timestamps here.

Actually, let me be more precise about the timing. The log file was last modified at 09:07 CST (01:07 UTC on May 14), so about 7.5 hours have passed since training started. At 1850 steps in 7.5 hours, that's roughly 247 steps per hour. With 2392 steps remaining to reach 4242, the first run should finish in about 9.7 more hours, totaling around 17.2 hours per run. That means 9 runs would take roughly 155 hours per GPU.

Now I'm checking this against the roadmap estimates. With 2 epochs instead of 3, the calculation changes to about 103 hours per GPU, which aligns with the roadmap's "~100-126h" range. If I trace through the timeline: the first run finishes around May 14 10:47 UTC, then 8 more runs at 11.4 hours each adds another 91.2 hours, putting completion around May 18 06:10 UTC. That matches the roadmap's "May 18-19" target, which is a good sign. The key assumption here is that eval@2000 will confirm overfitting and trigger the switch to 2 epochs.

The roadmap correctly flags this as "还没确认" — we're still waiting on that eval@2000 checkpoint at step 1850 to make the final call. Once that's confirmed, the remaining 8 seeds drop to 2 epochs as planned.

I'm also noting the statistical power concern the roadmap mentions: with n=3, we're looking at df=2 and higher Type II error, which means the discussion section will need to address this limitation carefully.

For the Plan_014 ablation, the roadmap says the D025 script is ready with 6 runs taking about 70 hours, but with dual-GPU parallelization that becomes 35 hours wall time. We're just waiting for M2 to free up GPU capacity before we can start that.

I'm realizing there's a potential GPU conflict though — the script I looked at earlier had `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES=0`, which would clash with the m2_full_qwen job already running on cuda:0. I should verify whether that's been resolved in the actual updated script, though my main focus here is checking whether the overall research direction and timeline make sense rather than auditing every implementation detail.

Looking at the timeline more carefully, there's a real crunch: M2 should wrap up around May 18 with the reduced epochs, plan_014 needs another 35 hours or so, then plan_003 and plan_004 still need to run before the May 25 deadline. That's only about 5-6 days to fit everything in, which is tight.

There's also the question of whether m1_reextract_v2 will produce enough new data to warrant re-running M2, but given the timeline constraints, that's probably not feasible before the ARR deadline anyway — the ROADMAP should be clearer about accepting this limitation.

I'm noticing that paper_framing.md still has the old pilot numbers that need to be swapped out for the actual M2 results once they're ready. And looking at the task list, Plan_015 and Plan_017 show up in the reviewer concerns section but aren't in the near-term work — I need to clarify whether those are actually deprioritized or just dropped entirely.

Now I'm cross-checking the ROADMAP claims against what's actually in the registry to make sure the numbers line up.

For Qwen and StarCoder, the reported metrics match the recorded values. The M1 reextract task is actively running with a progress estimate that might be slightly outdated based on what I saw in the logs earlier. The SWE-bench pipeline passed its dry-run as expected. For the temp ablation, I should verify the script was actually reduced from 9 runs to 6, though it's not critical for this review.

Looking at the overall assessment, the ROADMAP is well-structured and aligns with the evidence. The GPU allocation checks out, but there's a gap: Plans 015, 016, and 017 are mentioned in reviewer feedback yet missing from the near-term tasks—if they've been cut, the paper should explicitly acknowledge this limitation, especially Plan 015 which addresses the static versus dynamic mining confound that multiple reviewers flagged. The static versus dynamic mining distinction is a critical vulnerability—if we drop dynamic mining, the paper needs explicit language acknowledging this limitation. There's also ambiguity around how m1_reextract_v2 data integrates with M2 training, since the reextracted dataset could potentially double the current 15.9K structural samples, but the ROADMAP doesn't specify timing or methodology. Additionally, plan_014's GPU budget still shows 10 hours in the experiment config despite the recent updates.
```

```text
Looking at plan_002, the registry points to m2_embed_verify as a pilot, but the full M2 variants aren't independently tracked in the experiment plan—they're treated as extensions rather than separate entries, which could create gaps in coverage accounting. I'm noticing that plan_015's dynamic mining approach has been flagged by reviewers but hasn't made it into the near-term task list, so I should acknowledge in the paper's discussion section that we're working with static mining constraints if time doesn't allow. Also, I need to clarify where the m1_reextract_v2 data will be used—whether it's for the camera-ready version or future submissions.
```
